(d) Robustness and tumor-type subgroup estimates. Predictions were SHA-256-sealed before outcome access; the primary macro correlation was 0.1015, 17/20 target estimates were positive, and the two-sided within-tumor-type donor-level permutation p-value was 0.00040. In (a), arrows indicate cohort selection. In (b), blue, gray, and orange points and error bars denote expression-only, lineage-only, and expression-minus-lineage estimates, respectively. In (c), gray points denote q ≥ 0.05, and error bars use the corresponding point colors. In (d), filled circles denote cohort-level estimates, with the primary estimate in blue and the sensitivity and lineage-only reference estimates in gray; open squares denote tumor-type subgroup estimates. Black dashed vertical lines in (b–d) mark zero. BH, Benjamini–Hochberg; CI, confidence interval; SHA-256, 256-bit Secure Hash Algorithm.
As shown in Figure 5a,b, the frozen expression-only model achieved a macro target-wise Spearman correlation of 0.1015 in the independent Sanger-148 cohort (95% CI, 0.0637–0.1357). Seventeen of the 20 target estimates were positive. After repeated organoids were averaged within donors, 5000 two-sided permutations of complete 20-target outcome vectors within tumor-type strata yielded p = 0.00040. Thus, the locked 2D model carried a detectable rank-ordering signal into the independent 3D cohort, although the effect size was modest.
As shown in Figure 5c and Table S13, the largest target-specific correlations were observed for CDK6 (ρ = 0.3221; 95% CI, 0.1666–0.4702), MDM4 (ρ = 0.2992; 95% CI, 0.1483–0.4410), KEAP1 (ρ = 0.2967; 95% CI, 0.1256–0.4464), CTNNB1 (ρ = 0.2831; 95% CI, 0.1031–0.4563), and CCNE1 (ρ = 0.2792; 95% CI, 0.1014–0.4493). After Benjamini–Hochberg correction of the 20 target-level permutation p-values, only CDK6, MDM4, and CCNE1 met q < 0.05 (q = 0.0040 for each). CCND3, EGFR, and NFE2L2 were the three targets with negative correlations; no targets were removed based on direction or significance.
The tumor-type-adjusted macro Spearman correlation was 0.0882, whereas the frozen lineage-only reference achieved 0.0553 (95% CI, 0.0168–0.0917). The expression-only-minus-lineage difference was +0.0462, but its paired donor-cluster bootstrap interval included zero (95% CI, −0.0065 to 0.0986). The primary target-wise endpoint therefore did not establish superiority beyond tumor-origin information. The one-model-per-donor estimate (ρ = 0.1014) and donor-mean estimate (ρ = 0.0990) closely matched the primary result. Tumor-type subgroup estimates were 0.1030 for colorectal, 0.0654 for esophageal, 0.1755 for ovarian, 0.0300 for pancreatic, and 0.1500 for gastric organoids (Figure 5d). Ovarian results were exploratory, whereas pancreatic and gastric estimates were descriptive because of their small sample sizes.
The correlation between the vectors of target-specific Broad and Sanger performance estimates was ρ = 0.2782 (two-sided p = 0.2350), indicating that panel-wide directional replication did not imply stable relative performance ordering across cohorts (Figure 6b). The expression-only-minus-lineage differences were positive at the panel level in Broad-94 (one-sided Wilcoxon p = 0.0379) but did not reach the corresponding threshold in Sanger-148 (p = 0.1012). Thus, the retrospective synthesis strengthened evidence for limited cross-cohort directionality but did not constitute a third external validation or support equivalent target performance across the two organoid resources.
Fifteen of the 20 tumor-normal model-output contrasts met Benjamini–Hochberg q < 0.05 (Figure 7b and Table S15). The largest tumor-directed difference was observed for CTNNB1 (tumor minus normal, −0.435; 95% bootstrap CI, −0.564 to −0.298; q = 0.00439). CDK6 also showed a more negative tumor projection (−0.200; 95% CI, −0.243 to −0.159; q = 2.09 × 10−5), and CCNE1 showed a smaller difference in the same direction (−0.027; 95% CI, −0.051 to −0.009; q = 0.0260). By contrast, MDM4 was more negative in normal cases (tumor minus normal, +0.245; 95% CI, +0.217 to +0.277; q = 1.17 × 10−5). These p and q values describe differences in frozen model outputs and do not demonstrate measured gene dependency or therapeutic sensitivity.
Computational replay confirmed that the reference Tiriac projections used training-median imputation. Relative to these unchanged projections, zero filling produced a mean absolute change of 0.0114 and a maximum change of 0.1077 Gene Effect units; training-reference mean filling produced corresponding changes of 0.0017 and 0.0172. Across the 38 tumor-case profiles, mean within-profile Spearman agreement with the reference was 0.9771 for zero filling and 0.9991 for training-reference means. The top-three set was preserved in 36/38 and 38/38 tumor cases, respectively (Table S20). Zero filling therefore altered the short candidate list in two tumor cases despite high overall rank agreement.
The largest zero-fill shift occurred for RAF1 (−0.1077), whereas the largest training-reference mean shift occurred for CTNNB1 (−0.0172; Table S21). The tumor-median top-three hierarchy remained CTNNB1, CDK6 and YAP1 under both alternatives. Tumor–normal median differences and bootstrap limits changed by less than 0.000001; Mann–Whitney p-values, adjusted q-values, and the 15 significant projection contrasts were unchanged. This invariance follows from the common target-specific offsets in the linear models and does not independently validate the projections. The sensitivity analysis does not identify the most accurate imputation method because Tiriac lacks measured dependency outcomes.
Using the unchanged 20-gene predictions, the mean within-organoid Spearman correlation was 0.3428 (95% CI, 0.3026 to 0.3826) in Broad-94 and 0.4294 (95% CI, 0.3926 to 0.4645) in Sanger-148 (Table S17). The corresponding fixed 2D-mean comparator estimates were 0.2057 and 0.1995. Paired expression-minus-fixed-mean differences were +0.1371 (95% CI, 0.0987 to 0.1780) and +0.2299 (95% CI, 0.1981 to 0.2613), respectively. These estimates address gene ordering within each organoid and are not directly comparable to the original macro target-wise correlations of 0.1518 and 0.1015.
The archived lineage-only predictions yielded mean within-organoid correlations of 0.2745 in Broad and 0.3884 in Sanger. Expression-minus-lineage differences were +0.0684 (95% CI, 0.0334 to 0.1032) and +0.0411 (95% CI, 0.0167 to 0.0656), respectively. Thus, full-panel gene ranking showed a modest retrospective gain over lineage-only prediction in both cohorts (Table S17). This finding does not change the uncertainty in the original Sanger target-wise expression-minus-lineage difference.
This study tested whether transcriptome-based gene-dependency rankings learned in 2D cancer models retained a detectable but attenuated signal in patient-derived 3D organoids. Transfer was evaluated without outcome-guided retraining. The findings provide limited support for this hypothesis. We selected the expression-only representation in the development cohort. Holdout performance provided internal confirmation conditional on historical target selection. Positive panel-wide rank signals were observed in the Broad-94 exploratory cohort and the Sanger-148 blinded independent external validation cohort. However, macro correlations in Broad, Sanger, and the combined 3D analysis were markedly lower than in the locked 2D holdout. Transfer was therefore incomplete and weak. The findings support modest, context-sensitive preservation of organoid ranking for genes within the preselected panel across independently generated resources. They do not establish cross-platform equivalence. This primary finding is distinct from the secondary within-organoid gene-ranking results in Section 3.7.
For the prespecified target-wise endpoint, the added predictive value of transcriptomic modeling over tumor lineage was not established in the blinded Sanger cohort. The expression-only model exceeded the frozen lineage-only comparator numerically by 0.0462 in macro Spearman correlation. However, the paired donor-cluster bootstrap CI included zero (95% CI, −0.0065 to 0.0986). This result establishes neither superiority nor equivalence. The significant within-lineage permutation result addresses a different question: whether the prediction–outcome association exceeds a null distribution that preserves tumor-type structure. It does not establish superior performance over the lineage-only predictor. Target-specific correlations rank organoids for a given gene. They cannot by themselves establish correct gene ranking within an organoid or an increase in the yield of functional experiments. The study contributes an outcome-blinded assessment of performance attenuation from cell lines to organoids and documents uncertainty in the incremental value of transcriptomic prediction. CDK6, MDM4, and CCNE1 remain candidates for prospective investigation. Their multiplicity-adjusted correlations do not establish target-specific superiority over lineage or practical benefit.
Performance was lower in the organoid cohorts than in the 2D internal confirmation analysis. The macro Spearman correlation was 0.4094 in the locked 2D holdout, compared with 0.1518 in Broad-94 and 0.1015 in Sanger-148. This attenuation is interpreted as an observed cross-domain effect whose biological and technical causes remain uncertain. Computational alignment studies have documented transcriptional differences between cell lines and tumors [20]. Next-generation model resources have reported tumor-relevant transcriptional programs and culture-specific dependencies in organoids and spheroids [38], while patient-derived model collections have shown that culture conditions can modify cellular states in a subset of models [40]. These studies provide context for plausible explanations, but they do not establish the causes of the performance decline in the present cohorts. Differences in cellular state, growth geometry, medium composition, lineage composition, and functional-screening platforms may contribute individually or jointly. Their contributions were not experimentally disentangled or quantified here. The comparison used different cohorts and assay settings, rather than paired models in which growth conditions and screening platforms were varied under controlled conditions. It therefore cannot attribute the decline to 3D growth itself, identify a dominant mechanism, or exclude contributions from model limitations and cohort composition. Historical target-panel selection also limits interpretation of the 2D reference estimate. The observed attenuation supports evaluating dependency models across data domains without assigning a confirmed biological or technical explanation to the decline.
The retrospective missing-feature sensitivity analysis showed high overall agreement with the original median-imputed Tiriac projections. However, zero filling changed the top-three set in two of 38 tumor cases, and individual target shifts reached 0.1077 Gene Effect units. Training-reference mean filling preserved all tumor-case top-three sets, but this agreement cannot establish greater accuracy. Constant per-target offsets explain the unchanged tumor–normal contrasts. These findings support reporting the preprocessing rule explicitly and retaining the projections as hypotheses for functional testing (Tables S20 and S21).
The computational framework may help select direct organoid experiments, but its utility requires prospective testing. Genome-scale or large-scale CRISPR studies in intestinal, colon, and gastric organoids have identified context-specific genes related to growth, treatment resistance, and gene-drug interactions [34,35,36,37]. The larger Sanger organoid biobank has likewise mapped dependencies linked to genomic and clinical features through direct perturbation [39]. Such experiments provide stronger evidence of functional causality than computational projections, but remain technically demanding and resource-intensive. Frozen expression models could be evaluated as a low-cost pre-screen for allocating scarce CRISPR or pharmacological validation capacity. The within-organoid analysis tests the relative ordering of measured scores within 20 genes. It does not establish a common calibrated dependency scale across independently fitted gene models. Its modest full-ranking gains and cohort-dependent top-three results require prospective confirmation before claiming improved target prioritization.
This study shows that transcriptome-based models learned in 2D cancer models retain a weak ability to rank patient-derived 3D organoids by dependency for genes in the preselected panel, without retraining and with substantial attenuation. More complex omic representations did not provide a reliable advantage over the expression-only model. Its macro Spearman correlation of 0.4094 in the locked 2D holdout provides internal confirmation conditional on historical target selection, not an unbiased generalization estimate. Performance decreased to 0.1518 in the Broad-94 exploratory cohort and to 0.1015 in the Sanger-148 blinded, independent external validation cohort. The Sanger association nevertheless exceeded the permutation-based null expectation. Eighteen of 20 target estimates were positive in Broad-94, 17 of 20 were positive in Sanger-148, and 15 of 20 were positive in both cohorts. The combined 3D macro correlation of 0.1231 therefore supports a modest, directionally reproducible rank-transfer signal rather than complete cross-system generalizability. The primary endpoint did not establish added predictive value beyond tumor lineage in Sanger. These results support transfer of predictive associations without establishing their causal basis. Broad uncertainty and retrospective pooled evidence remain conditional on incomplete donor ascertainment. The attenuation is an observed cross-domain effect; its biological and technical causes were not experimentally disentangled and remain uncertain.

Sign-flip rates for test genes (medium 90.8%, format 98.4%) match 750 controls (92.1%, 98.1%) and zero-effect noise expectations. Magnitude evidence: median |Δ| medium 0.183 (test) vs 0.164 (controls); mean |mean Δ| 0.137 vs 0.094 — modestly larger test-gene effects, consistent with selection, not proof of heterogeneity.     21	## Preprocessing dependency graphs (audited from source text/code)
    22	- **Broad genome-wide Chronos:** all NextGen screens used Humagne library → library batch **confounded** with organoid-ness; authors pre-trained Chronos on traditional + NextGen **CNS** models, then applied parameters to organoids. Consequence: NextGen Chronos values (incl. the organoids-vs-2D table, n=94 vs 433) are not outcome-independent of NextGen data; usable as **development** evidence, never as independent validation of Broad-derived selections. Minipool Chronos computed "in the same manner" on enAsCas12a screens (separate library); within-model paired contrasts share processing within pairs.
    23	- **Sanger:** pipeline = CRISPRcleanR (per-sample CN correction) → spline normalization (expression bias) → **ComBat batch correction across samples** → BAGEL2 binary calls (verified in `Garnett-Lab/SangerOrganoidBiobank/scripts/CRISPR_pipeline.R`). ComBat/spline parameters are fitted across the cohort's outcomes → processed Sanger matrices are **not** development-independent by construction. Authoritative processed route per S2: Cell Model Passports + DepMap Miner; figshare holds figure-reproduction files incl. `07_EssMatrix_qnorm_corrected_logFCs.RData` (continuous LFCs; **not downloaded**) and BAGEL binary depletions (downloaded, quarantined; 17,647×930 ACH — a DepMap-wide binary matrix, **not** itself the 162-organoid continuous outcome). Confirmatory use requires, at Lock 1/2: cohort identity via crosswalk, duplicate-library rule (2 libraries/model in places), and either development-only refit from counts (`sgRNAs-counts…csv` exists on figshare) or explicit downgrade of independence claims. Binary BAGEL ≠ continuous rank endpoint; never substituted silently.
    24	- Assay scales (Chronos vs corrected LFC) remain non-interchangeable; cross-study = rank/calibration only.
    25	
    26	## Exposure adjudication (final)
    27	Glimpsed cells (from retained transcript, file not reopened): genes `A1BG, A1CF, A2M` × models `ACH-001421, ACH-001412, ACH-000888, ACH-001184, ACH-000317, ACH-000264`. All 6 models are **traditional 2D cell lines** (Broad metadata, `IsNextGen=False`); none of the 3 genes is in the 250 panel. **Zero candidate Layer-C organoid outcomes were exposed.** Disposition: organoid-cohort blinding intact with permanent disclosure (`data/exposure_log.jsonl`); no exclusions required; BAGEL file stays quarantined; any future use of those 6 cell lines as development data must cite this log.
    28	
    29	## Partitions (corrected)
    30	Layer A = Broad study-specific figshare (inspected; development). Layer C candidates = Sanger outcomes (quarantined; schema-only). **Layer B is NOT carved from inspected minipool outcomes** — B panels are *task definitions for fresh Phase-5 rollouts whose outcomes do not yet exist*; any policy evaluation reusing already-inspected observations is labeled **reused-data engineering evaluation**, not reserved/untouched. Exposure history ships with every split manifest.
    31	
    32	## Remaining G1-open (honest)
    33	Donor identities for the 7 minipool models + HCM-SANG↔ACH crosswalk (routes: DepMap Model.csv interactive download; S3 Zenodo registries at Phase 2); genome-wide Broad matrix (interactive portal route); Sanger continuous-matrix identity confirmation via Passports/Miner docs. None blocks the narrowed development estimand in `reviews/g1_question_v2.md`; each gates the specific claims noted there.
     1	# Novelty audit v1 — 2026-10-28 (Phase 1f; refresh required at G7 + terminal determination)
     2	
     3	## Method
     4	Full-text reads (Europe PMC XML, `data/papers/`): S1 PMC13581591, S2 PMC13581617, S3 PMC13664187. Dated Europe PMC searches 2026-10-28 (queries preserved in session record): culture/CRISPR/dependency/organoid (7 hits), culture-conditions transfer (14 hits), medium/essentiality 2025–2026 (176 hits, top reviewed). **Absence of a search hit is not proof of novelty** — claims below are candidates pending G1 review.
     5	
     6	## Already established (controls / priors — NOT novelty)
     7	1. **S1 (Broad NextGen):** class-level environmental effects in the 250-gene minipool — integrin/actin classes vary by **format** (plastic vs dome), lipid-metabolism class by **medium** (RPMI-FBS vs OPAC), via OLS on 18 screens/7 models. Also: 147 genome-scale NextGen screens, biomarker dependencies, CDK6/CDKN2A-GBM work.
     8	2. **S2 (Sanger biobank):** 162-organoid dependency map, biomarker associations, organoid-specific essentials.
     9	3. **S3 (Oct 2026 transfer study):** expression-only Elastic Net (20 genes) transfer quantification 2D→organoids (Broad-94 ρ=0.15, Sanger-148 ρ=0.10), beyond-lineage checks. **Explicitly did not disentangle** state/geometry/medium/lineage/platform contributions. Generic transfer prediction is therefore closed as a novelty claim.
    10	4. Adjacent: murine MYC 3D-vs-2D fitness programs (PMC12055005, 2025) — different system, no human dependency-transfer estimand.
    11	
    12	## Residual uncertainty (the gap)
    13	Nobody has tested whether **measured** per-gene environmental sensitivity (paired medium/format deltas with proper joint uncertainty and heterogeneity) **explains which dependencies fail to transfer** — i.e., whether environment-labile genes are exactly where 2D→organoid relationships (dependency levels and/or biomarker associations) degrade, beyond lineage and baseline strength. S1 measured sensitivity (7 models, class-level report); S3 measured transfer failure (20 genes, no environmental attribution). The link, with correct independent-unit counting and heterogeneity-aware estimands, is unreported in the searched literature.
    14	
    15	Our audit adds a sharpening fact: per-gene average deltas hide extreme heterogeneity (91–98% of test genes flip sign across model pairs). Any credible claim must therefore be **conditional** (which genes × which model contexts) — class-average or mean-effect versions are likely unidentifiable/misleading at n=7 models. Precision-supported **refutations/bounds** (e.g., "average environmental sensitivity does not explain transfer degradation beyond X") are acceptable G1 targets.
    16	
    17	## Candidate claim ledger (exploratory; Lock 1 will bound the family)
    18	- **CL-1 (primary candidate):** Among the 250 panel genes, transfer degradation of dependency behavior (traditional→NextGen organoid, Layer-A genome-wide data) increases with measured environmental sensitivity magnitude/heterogeneity, conditional on lineage + baseline dependency strength. *Falsifier:* no association (or reversed) under joint-uncertainty propagation and strongest-baseline controls. *Needs:* Layer-A genome-wide import, joint bootstrap, matched controls.
    19	- **CL-2 (conditional form):** The CL-1 association is concentrated in specific gene classes/contexts (e.g., medium-labile lipid genes vs format-labile adhesion genes behave differently in transfer). *Risk:* n=7 models ⇒ classes, not genes, may be the finest honest resolution.
    20	- **CL-3 (bound/refutation candidate):** After conditioning, environmental sensitivity explains < δ of transfer degradation (informative bound redirecting interpretation toward lineage/state/platform). Qualifies only with predeclared δ + sufficient precision.
    21	- **Excluded as novelty:** re-deriving S1 class effects; re-running S3's 20-gene transfer; any average-effect "environment matters" statement without heterogeneity/conditioning.
    22	
    23	## Open items for G1 gate
    24	S1/S2 supplement + author-code deep read; Zenodo S3 code inspection; Layer-A genome-wide route decision; crosswalk completion; sol-high review of this audit + provenance (G1 gate requires ≥ 90).

 succeeded in 806ms:
 (f_g(X_i)-f_g(X_j))(Y_{ig}-Y_{jg})>0
 \mid i,j\text{ distinct donors in group }\ell
 \right],
\]

with ties receiving half credit. Then define transfer loss:

\[
d_g=C_g^R-C_g^C.
\]

Positive values mean worse ordering in organoids. This compares ordering accuracy without subtracting Chronos from Sanger LFC.

The **single primary parameter** is the finite-panel linear-projection coefficient

\[
\boxed{\displaystyle
\beta=
\frac{a^\top M_Xd}{a^\top M_Xa},
\qquad a_g=s_g/\operatorname{IQR}(s).
}
\]

\(M_X\) removes the fixed baseline covariates: source dependency mean and SD, source ordering accuracy, signed and absolute Broad selection-table `MeanDifference`, and the library’s functional-class indicators. Use their fixed column space; overlapping classes do not justify adaptive variable selection.

**Interpretation:** additional loss of correct donor ordering per one-IQR increase in measured format lability, conditional on those baselines, this target family and these environmental supports.

The predictor comparison must include lineage-only, genotype-only, S3-style expression Elastic Net, and genotype-plus-expression models, using the same permitted observations. Select the predictor family and tune it entirely within \(T\), then freeze it before evaluating \(R\) and \(C\). Environmental scores enter the explanatory association, not external predictor selection.

This route still needs the individual-level source matrix. Published group means cannot supply \(C_g^R\).

**2. Selection circularity: exact exclusion plus conditional external evaluation**

The downloaded trace supports this reproducible rule:

1. In `organoids_vs_2d_gene_dependency_tests_full.csv`, retain `DropEssential=False`.
2. Sort by signed `MeanDifference`.
3. Take the lowest 100 and highest 100 genes. **Do not substitute an FDR cutoff.**
4. Match the table’s `SYMBOL (EntrezID)` identifiers to library genes.
5. Exclude their intersection with `All test genes=True`.

That removes **26 of 250 test genes**, leaving **224** before other eligibility exclusions. The tail boundaries have no ties.

However, **224 does not mean selection-independent genes**. Whole functional classes were chosen using the same comparison. The [library documentation](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/cancer-dependency-rsi/data/raw/broad_figshare/README.txt:1) and enrichment trace make that pathway-level selection explicit.

The remaining protection is therefore:

- Condition the claim on the entire historical panel-selection process and fixed family.
- Use the Broad comparison table only as development information and frozen baseline covariates.
- Obtain the evaluation outcome from Sanger donors excluded from all overlapping Broad environmental, selection, training and reference cohorts.
- Exclude uncertain donor matches from confirmatory eligibility.
- Establish that Sanger evaluation outcomes did not influence any source predictor, selection artifact or environmental-score processing artifact.

This can neutralize **reuse of the selection outcome as confirmation**. It cannot identify a genome-wide, selection-unconditional association. Adjusting for `MeanDifference` alone would not establish independence.

**3. Independent units and achievable precision**

Format supplies **seven contrasts across six models**, at most six independent environmental donors pending crosswalk completion. Medium supplies five contrasts across four models; restricting medium to dome gives four clean supports.

The 224 genes are correlated targets. They are not 224 environmental experiments.

There are two different inferential ambitions:

- **Conditional on the six measured environmental supports:** a panel-wide association is feasible in principle. External donors provide repeated evaluations of a fixed vector of measured lability scores. Cross-target dependence must be retained by resampling complete donor outcome vectors.
- **For latent biological sensitivity or new environmental models:** technical measurement error and environmental-model sampling matter. Neither is resolved by having many genes. With collapsed screen estimates and unaudited replicate uncertainty, the latent-sensitivity coefficient remains unidentified.

A rough precision check illustrates the limitation. If a model-cluster influence SD were \(s_u\), an approximate 95% half-width would be:

\[
t_{.975,5}s_u/\sqrt6\approx1.05s_u
\]

for format, versus \(1.59s_u\) for four-model medium. An influence SD of 0.10 already produces approximately ±0.105 for format, before other uncertainty. These are feasibility illustrations, not calibrated intervals for deliberately selected models.

For external ordering accuracy, under a simple independent-donor null, its SE is approximately \(1/(3\sqrt N)\): about 0.027 at 150 donors. A difference between two equally sized domains has SE around 0.038 per target under those assumptions. Donor pairs are dependent; their quadratic count does not provide quadratic precision.

A shared coefficient can be more precise than individual target losses, but that depends on the **actual joint covariance**, residual lability variation after conditioning, and environmental-score uncertainty.

**Verdict:** a conditional panel-wide parameter is plausible. “Only a bound” is not forced by \(n=6\), and a useful bound is not guaranteed. No actual precision claim is defensible before the eligible donor counts and joint uncertainty are established. A bound on *true biological sensitivity* additionally needs a defensible measurement-error model.

**4. Sanger’s permitted role**

Sanger can provide external evidence for the frozen association after establishing continuous-outcome identities, target coverage, donor crosswalks, library selection and processing provenance. Do not inherit S3’s cohort count as this study’s eligible count.

Its pipeline fits spline corrections using cross-sample LFC means and applies ComBat across libraries. Those operations use outcomes and can alter ranks. If fitted wholly within the external cohort, they do not automatically contaminate Broad training, but they couple external observations and require processing-aware uncertainty or a clearly conditional processed-cohort interpretation. If evaluation outcomes influenced development artifacts, the affected processing must be reconstructed or replaced. [Sanger processing code](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Garnett-Lab/SangerOrganoidBiobank/main/scripts/CRISPR_pipeline.R).

The quarantined BAGEL matrix cannot supply this continuous ordering endpoint. Its 930-column schema also does not establish the intended organoid cohort.

Recover the 18 displayed cell identifiers from retained transcripts without reopening values. Map them to donors and targets. Conservatively exclude implicated external donors, record target exposure, and assess whether that exposure influenced family or endpoint choices. Unrecoverable exposure prevents an intact outcome-unseen claim; it does not automatically invalidate the entire resource.

Sanger cannot identify a causal culture contribution to transfer loss. Platform, library, population and processing differences remain alternative explanations.

**5. Falsification, inconclusive and invalid-result rules**

Propose **\(\delta=0.10\) ordering-accuracy loss per lability IQR**.

Its justification is experimental prioritization: ten additional incorrect orderings per 100 donor comparisons materially degrade a ranking tool; a drop from 0.60 accuracy to 0.50 removes its useful discrimination. This is a proposed decision-based margin, not an established clinical threshold. Its utility rationale must be accepted before evaluation and cannot be enlarged to match attainable precision.

Using one prespecified joint 95% interval \([L,U]\):

| Result | Decision |
|---|---|
| \(L>0.10\) | Support the practically meaningful positive association. |
| \(U<0.10\) | Falsify the claim of an association at least this large. |
| Entire interval lies inside \((-0.10,0.10)\) | Support practical equivalence for this conditional parameter. |
| \(U<-0.10\) | Support a practically meaningful association in the opposite direction. |
| \(L\le0.10\le U\) | Inconclusive about the primary meaningful-positive claim. |

A small positive estimate can therefore falsify the meaningful-positive claim. A nonsignificant estimate with a wide interval cannot.

Additional rules:

- Unresolved provenance, donor identity, score direction or joins: **invalid for confirmation**.
- No demonstrated above-chance source prediction: **no established source relationship whose transfer can be assessed**.
- Zero residual lability variation after baseline conditioning: parameter undefined.
- Material dependence on one environmental model or plausible measurement-error assumptions: no robust biological interpretation; report that limitation.
- One primary coefficient receives the confirmatory decision. Target-specific, pathway and medium analyses remain exploratory.

A bound here concerns the usefulness of the **measured lability score** for explaining this transfer endpoint. It does not bound the total causal contribution of culture conditions.

**6. Novelty sentence**

*The candidate contribution is a selection-conditioned estimate, or informative bound, of the association between measured plastic-to-Matrigel-dome dependency lability and independently evaluated loss of within-lineage donor-ordering accuracy from frozen molecular dependency predictors.*

That distinguishes the estimand from S1’s environmental findings, S2’s dependency/biomarker maps and S3’s transfer evaluation. It remains a candidate novelty claim until the outstanding supplement/code audit checks this exact relationship.

Taking this route requires recording the following limits against the ambitious goal:

- Drop causal attribution to culture, isolated geometry/serum effects, and genotype × state × context discovery.
- Drop genome-wide and new-environmental-model generalization.
- Drop intrinsic heterogeneity and invariance claims unsupported by replicate-level uncertainty.
- Retain an atlas of all 250 test genes, explicitly marking the 26 primary-family exclusions and every subsequent exclusion.
- Keep **C6 unmet** until an externally supported finding or sufficiently precise bound actually passes.
- Keep **C8/C9 unmet**: this narrowed study does not establish policy improvement or 20% resource savings, and exposed Layer A cannot become untouched Layer B through a later split.
- Record that a G1 design approval would not complete the broader explanatory goal or the RSI objective.

No files were edited, and no additional Sanger outcome values were inspected.

Two further checks matter for the verdict: eight joined genes have fewer than 433 adherent observations, and `TMOD` is a documented alias of `TMOD1`, which is present in the comparison table. The claimed attrition needs identifier adjudication. I also reproduced 0.085695 as Spearman correlation after re-ranking the rank residuals; the conventional Pearson correlation of those residuals is 0.083961, so Lock 1 needs to specify the operation.

codex
**G1 verdict: FAIL.** V2 establishes a computable, panel-conditional **descriptive association**, but still does not establish the transfer question it claims to test. This failure concerns identification and evidence—not unfinished Lock-1 implementation or the broader project’s completion criteria.

I read all requested materials. Independent Broad checks reproduced the matrix dimensions, contrast counts, selection exclusions, sign-flip statistics, direction checks, and raw correlation. All 18 manifested files match their recorded sizes and SHA-256 hashes and the saved Figshare responses’ file checksums. Live Figshare API verification was unavailable. No quarantined Sanger outcomes were parsed or displayed, and no edits were made.

1. **R1-1 [CRITICAL] Operational estimand — PARTIALLY RESOLVED.**  
   [Question v2](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/cancer-dependency-rsi/reviews/g1_question_v2.md:10) now defines `T_g = |MeanDifference_g|` and a partial Spearman association. The raw association reproduces exactly: **ρ = 0.1668304275**.

   However, absolute organoid–adherent **mean dependency difference is not loss of predictive transfer**. A relationship can transfer perfectly despite a mean shift, or fail without one. The external endpoint remains “**continuous corrected LFC ranking endpoint, exact form at Lock 1**.” Its reference, comparator, and numerical transfer-loss quantity are still unspecified. Those are identification choices, not merely implementation details.

2. **R1-2 [CRITICAL] Selection circularity — PARTIALLY RESOLVED.**  
   [Provenance v2](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/cancer-dependency-rsi/data/provenance_v2.md:18) reports “**26/250 panel genes are direct 200-outlier members**.” I independently reproduced the exact 26-gene exclusion list using the specified tails.

   The restriction “**Broad-side associations are development/descriptive evidence only**” is appropriate. Nevertheless, pathway-level selection remains, and the primary response still comes from the comparison that informed panel construction. Excluding direct tail members does not make the remaining association selection-independent. Circularity is adequately contained **for a descriptive development statistic**, but not resolved for the claimed independent transfer finding. Donor-disjoint external evidence remains unestablished.

3. **R1-3 [CRITICAL] Preprocessing independence — PARTIALLY RESOLVED.**  
   The audit now identifies concrete transformations, but its conclusion that Sanger matrices are “**not development-independent by construction**” is too strong. The [author pipeline](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Garnett-Lab/SangerOrganoidBiobank/main/scripts/CRISPR_pipeline.R) computes cross-sample mean LFCs for spline correction and applies ComBat across libraries. This establishes outcome-dependent coupling within the processed cohort; it does **not**, by itself, establish influence on Broad development artifacts.

   The missing evidence remains the actual fitting populations and artifact dependencies across the development/evaluation boundary. Conversely, merely “downgrading independence” cannot repair genuine cross-boundary leakage. The Broad minipool processing description also remains insufficient to identify its fitted parameter dependencies.

4. **R1-4 [HIGH] Independent-unit counts and weighting — RESOLVED for the counting error.**  
   [The audit](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/cancer-dependency-rsi/data/identifiability_audit.json:1) correctly reports **5 medium pairs/4 models** and **7 format pairs/6 models**. Both reproduce. Question v2 explicitly averages ACH-003040’s two contrasts before assigning equal model weights.

   “**Donors … UNRESOLVED**” appropriately prevents equating six models with six verified independent donors. Donor ascertainment remains an external-eligibility requirement, but the original counting and weighting mistake is corrected.

5. **R1-5 [HIGH] Unsupported heterogeneity interpretation — PARTIALLY RESOLVED.**  
   [Provenance v2](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/cancer-dependency-rsi/data/provenance_v2.md:14) explicitly says “**heterogeneity dominates / averages near-uninterpretable … retracted**.” The test/control sign-flip and magnitude statistics reproduce.

   Yet the still-operative [novelty audit](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/cancer-dependency-rsi/reviews/novelty_audit_v1.md:15) continues to assert “**per-gene average deltas hide extreme heterogeneity**,” using the same sign flips to motivate context stratification. The correction has not propagated through the package. Technical-noise calibration also remains absent.

6. **R1-6 [HIGH] Observation provenance and attrition — PARTIALLY RESOLVED.**  
   The new [35-row registry](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/cancer-dependency-rsi/data/observation_provenance_minipool.csv:1) materially improves replicate, condition, QC, and laboratory documentation. Missing lineage for ACH-003052 is explicitly acknowledged and appropriately limits lineage-conditioned claims.

   However, donor identities and biological sample origins remain incomplete, and missing condition combinations are not individually registered with their disposition. More seriously, “**1 replicate removed**” conflicts with the released sequence metadata, which flags **two** excluded replicates:

   - `0178_Adh_Alt_SF_Dome_RepA`
   - `0178_Adh_Nat_Serum_Dome_RepB`

   The registry copies both exclusion flags but annotates only the latter. Screen-level read totals include both flagged replicates’ reads. This requires reconciliation of QC summaries versus actual scoring inputs; it does not establish which replicates entered Chronos.

7. **R1-7 [HIGH] Treatment scope and confounding — RESOLVED.**  
   [V2](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/cancer-dependency-rsi/data/provenance_v2.md:9) correctly names the full medium-composition and plastic–Matrigel-dome bundles. Question v2 explicitly rejects “**causal explanation of transfer failure**” and isolated geometry/serum claims. Restricting interpretation to observed within-model comparisons is appropriate.

8. **R1-8 [HIGH] Sanger validation outcome identity — UNRESOLVED.**  
   The package now correctly distinguishes binary BAGEL calls from continuous outcomes. But [provenance v2](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/cancer-dependency-rsi/data/provenance_v2.md:23) still says the continuous matrix is “**not downloaded**,” while cohort identity, crosswalks, coverage, and duplicate-library disposition remain future work.

   Naming Passports/Miner and locating a continuous-file entry establishes a plausible route, not a demonstrated joinable evaluation cohort. Schema and identifier verification can proceed without inspecting quarantined outcome values; it has not yet been supplied.

9. **R1-9 [HIGH] Exposure disposition — RESOLVED for the documented 18-cell event.**  
   [The exposure log](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/cancer-dependency-rsi/data/exposure_log.jsonl:4) now names the three genes and six models. Independent Broad metadata checks confirm all six models have `IsTraditional2D=True`; none of the three genes belongs to the 250-gene panel.

   That supports retaining the candidate organoid evaluation rather than discarding the resource. It supports the narrow conclusion that the **listed exposed cells are outside the eligible target panel**. It does not certify that public outcomes were globally unknowable or that an as-yet-unresolved cohort is genuinely blinded.

10. **R1-10 [HIGH] Layer-B reuse — PARTIALLY RESOLVED.**  
    [Partitions v2](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/cancer-dependency-rsi/data/partitions_provisional.json:8) correctly labels inspected-data reuse “**reused-data engineering evaluation**.”

    However, “**task definitions for FRESH Phase-5 rollouts**” does not itself establish reserved biological evidence. New rollouts can generate new engineering outcomes while reusing known biological rewards. No actual reserved panel membership is demonstrated. This remains a policy-evaluation limitation, not an independent reason to fail the scientific G1 gate.

11. **R1-11 [HIGH] Precise novelty — UNRESOLVED.**  
    The [novelty audit](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/cancer-dependency-rsi/reviews/novelty_audit_v1.md:13) still says “**Nobody has tested**,” retains the rejected heterogeneity rationale, and lists supplement/code reads as “**Open items for G1 gate**.”

    More fundamentally, its proposed gap concerns environmental sensitivity versus **transfer degradation**, whereas the implemented statistic concerns environmental measurements versus **cohort mean differences**. That narrower descriptive relationship needs its own comparison against S1’s analyses. The current audit does not establish it as a distinct unresolved contribution.

12. **R1-12 [HIGH] Falsification, precision, and δ — PARTIALLY RESOLVED.**  
    The `SIGNAL`/`BOUND`/`INCONCLUSIVE` rules are substantially clearer. The [precision artifact](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/cancer-dependency-rsi/data/precision_feasibility_v1.json:14) honestly states that its model bootstrap captures “**environmental-side uncertainty only**.”

    Nevertheless:

    - The supplied interval **[0.00834, 0.24742] concerns raw ρ**, while the primary parameter is partial ρ.
    - It straddles δ = 0.20 and establishes neither a signal nor a bound.
    - No eligible external donor count or external-endpoint precision calculation is supplied.
    - Interval width alone does not establish that a meaningful signal is infeasible.
    - A bound below **+0.20** excludes a sufficiently large positive association; it does not establish weak association in both directions.

    **δ = 0.20 may remain a candidate for Lock 1, but its present justification is inadequate.** “Would not change target-trust decisions” lacks a specified decision rule. S3’s prediction–outcome correlations measure something different from this across-gene explanatory association. Finalizing δ later is permissible; treating its scientific relevance or attainability as established now is not.

13. **R1-13 [MEDIUM] Manifest completeness and reproducibility — PARTIALLY RESOLVED.**  
    Both formerly omitted Sanger files are now manifested. Saved authoritative responses include version and license information, and all 18 local files match their records. The revised audit code independently reproduces the reported direction checks and descriptive statistics.

    Remaining gaps include missing per-entry article versions for **9/13 Broad entries**, unpinned processing-code revisions, and no supplied regeneration procedure for the precision artifact. The documented QC discrepancy is useful, but the stated screen-read range “**6.7M–59M**” is incorrect: the released maximum is **88,316,766**.

The additional issues are consequential:

- **The claimed common reference population is inaccurate.** Among the 223 joined genes, 215 have `AdherentN=433`; five have **9**, two have **145**, and one has **432**. Thus the primary response mixes differently supported reference comparisons. Eligibility and uncertainty must address this explicitly.
- **The claimed TMOD attrition is not established.** The table contains `TMOD1 (7111)`, and [NCBI documents `TMOD` as its alias](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/7111/). The library guides require identifier adjudication before calling this missing outcome coverage. **223 exact-symbol joins is verified; 223 as the scientifically correct eligible population is provisional.**
- **The lability operator changed from the unstick recommendation.** Unstick averages absolute medium-specific changes; v2 takes the absolute value after averaging those changes within a model. These differ for **111/223 genes**, allowing opposing changes in ACH-003040 to cancel. Either can define a descriptive score, but they answer different questions.
- **“Partial Spearman” needs an exact definition.** The reported **0.085695** reproduces by correlating re-ranked rank residuals. Pearson correlation of the rank residuals gives **0.083961**. This small discrepancy is suitable for Lock-1 clarification, but the accompanying variance-explained argument must match the chosen operation.
- **The fallback changes the question again.** A “within-Broad environmental bound” does not follow automatically from an upper bound on association with absolute cohort mean differences. It cannot bound the causal contribution of culture or latent biological sensitivity.

**Operational and joinable:** yes, for the narrowly defined descriptive Broad statistic, subject to identifier adjudication and heterogeneous reference coverage. **Circularity:** appropriately disclosed for development description, inadequately controlled for independent confirmation. **Precision:** limitations are disclosed, but primary and external feasibility remain undemonstrated. **δ:** provisional, with insufficient decision justification.

A G1 pass does not require completed external evaluation or finalized Lock-1 machinery. It does require one coherent, plausibly novel question with a demonstrated evidence route. V2 still alternates among cohort mean shift, unspecified ranking transfer, and an environmental bound. The [unstick review](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/reviews/unstick_g1.md:15) explicitly warned: “**A correlation between minipool effects and the downloaded Broad group-comparison table … cannot clear the independent-transfer gate.**” V2 has implemented that development correlation without resolving the distinction.
